It is maintained by the University of Groningen.\n\n## Data Source\n- URL: https://www.rug.nl/ggdc/productivity/pwt/?lang=en\n- Current version: PWT 10.01 (as of 2023)\n- Format: Excel (.xlsx) file\n\n## Key Variables for Production Function Analysis\n\nFrom the PWT data dictionary:\n\n| Variable | Description |\n|----------|-------------|\n| `rgdpna` | Real GDP at constant 2017 national prices (in mil. 2017US$) |\n| `rkna` | Capital stock at constant 2017 national prices (in mil. 2017US$) |\n| `emp` | Number of persons engaged (in millions) |\n| `avh` | Average annual hours worked by persons engaged |\n| `hc` | Human capital index, based on years of schooling and returns to education |\n| `labsh` | Share of labour compensation in GDP at current national prices |\n| `ctfp` | TFP level at current PPPs (USA=1) |\n| `rtfpna` | TFP at constant national prices (2017=1) |\n| `delta` | Average depreciation rate of the capital stock |\n\n## Downloading the Data\n\n### Using wget/curl\n```bash\n# Download the latest PWT Excel file\nwget \"https://www.rug.nl/ggdc/productivity/pwt/downloads/pwt1001.xlsx\" -O pwt_data.xlsx\n```\n\n### Data Structure\nThe Excel file typically contains:\n1. **Data sheet**: Main data with all countries and years\n2. **Legend/Info sheet**: Variable definitions\n3. **Sources sheet**: Data sources information\n\n## Extracting Country-Specific Data\n\nFor a specific country like Georgia (country code: GEO):\n1. Filter by `countrycode` = \"GEO\"\n2. Select relevant years (e.g., 2000-2023)\n3. Extract needed variables: `rkna`, `rgdpna`, `labsh`, `delta`, etc.\n\n## Capital Stock Calculation\n\nThe capital stock (`rkna`) is calculated using the perpetual inventory method:\n```\nK(t) = I(t) + (1 - δ) * K(t-1)\n```\n\nWhere:\n- K(t) = Capital stock at time t\n- I(t) = Investment at time t\n- δ = Depreciation rate\n\n## Labor Share and Capital Share\n\nFor Cobb-Douglas production function:\n- Labor share (α) = `labsh` from PWT\n- Capital share (β) = 1 - `labsh`\n\nThe production function: Y = A * K^β * L^α\n\n## Usage in Excel\n\nAfter downloading:\n1. Open the PWT Excel file\n2. Use VLOOKUP or FILTER to extract Georgia data\n3. Link relevant columns to your analysis workbook\n\nExample Excel formula:\n```\n=VLOOKUP(\"GEO\"&B2, pwt_data!A:Z, column_number, FALSE)\n```\n\n## Notes for Georgia\n- Country code: GEO\n- Data typically available from early 1990s (post-Soviet period)\n- Capital stock data may have gaps in early years\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":13,"cache_creation_input_tokens":519,"cache_read_input_tokens":19496,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":519,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":25},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"e5187bd3-9f2d-48b6-b06d-731e911b28ca","uuid":"ce8f6b8e-e446-4a61-b56d-6d6cd6b791e8"}

Navigate to https://www.imf.org/en/Publications/WEO/weo-database\n2. Click on the latest WEO edition\n3. Select \"By Countries\" or \"By Subject\"\n4. For country-specific data:\n   - Select country: Georgia\n   - Choose subjects:\n     - \"Gross domestic product, constant prices\" (NGDP_R)\n     - \"Gross domestic product, constant prices, percent change\" (NGDPRPCH)\n   - Select years: 2000-2029 (includes projections)\n5. Download as Excel or copy data\n\n## Using Playwright for Automated Collection\n\n```javascript\n// Navigate to WEO database\nawait page.goto('https://www.imf.org/en/Publications/WEO/weo-database');\n\n// Click on latest edition\nawait page.click('text=October 2024'); // or current edition\n\n// Select \"By Countries\"\nawait page.click('text=By Countries');\n\n// Search and select Georgia\nawait page.fill('input[placeholder=\"Search\"]', 'Georgia');\nawait page.click('text=Georgia');\n\n// Select indicators\nawait page.click('text=National Accounts');\nawait page.click('text=Gross domestic product, constant prices');\n\n// Download or extract data\n```\n\n## Data Structure in WEO\n\nThe downloaded data typically includes:\n- Country name and ISO code\n- Subject descriptor\n- Units\n- Scale\n- Annual data columns (1980-2029)\n- Estimates and projections marked\n\n## Extending Projections\n\nFor years beyond IMF projections:\n1. Take the last projected growth rate\n2. Apply compound growth:\n```\nGDP(t) = GDP(t-1) * (1 + growth_rate)\n```\n\nExample Excel formula:\n```\n=B27*(1+$B$28)  // Where B27 is last GDP and B28 is constant growth rate\n```\n\n## Real GDP Calculations\n\nReal GDP level calculation from growth rates:\n```\nGDP_t = GDP_base * PRODUCT((1 + g_i) for i from base to t)\n```\n\nIn Excel:\n```\n=GDP_base * (1+growth_rate_1) * (1+growth_rate_2) * ...\n```\n\n## Notes for Georgia\n- Currency: Georgian Lari (GEL)\n- Real GDP typically in billions of national currency\n- IMF projections usually extend 5 years ahead\n- Historical data from ~1994 (post-independence)\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":13,"cache_creation_input_tokens":940,"cache_read_input_tokens":20015,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":940,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":24},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"e5187bd3-9f2d-48b6-b06d-731e911b28ca","uuid":"bb43987a-bfe6-4b8e-b5ff-d7e971dbec70"}

{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-5-20251101","id":"msg_vrtx_01GPBGQtMWhBCDn41P1SzBzV","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_vrtx_019tLpaaGuSshddEo954b3Js","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/root/environment/skills/ecb-data-access.md","content":"# ECB Statistical Data Warehouse Access\n\n## Overview\nThe European Central Bank (ECB) provides statistical data for various countries, including non-EU countries like Georgia through partner data sharing agreements.\n\n## Data Source\n- Main URL: https://data.ecb.europa.eu/\n- Georgia-specific: https://data.ecb.europa.eu/data/geographical-areas/georgia\n\n## Key Variables for This Analysis\n\n### Consumption of Fixed Capital (CFC)\n- Definition: The decline in value of fixed assets due to normal wear and tear and foreseeable obsolescence\n- Also known as: Depreciation\n- Used to calculate: Depreciation rate = CFC / Capital Stock\n\n## Navigating ECB Data Portal\n\n### Finding CFC Data for Georgia:\n1. Go to https://data.ecb.europa.eu/\n2. Navigate to \"Geographical Areas\" → \"Georgia\"\n3. Look for \"National Accounts\" section\n4. Find \"Consumption of fixed capital\" series\n\n### Data Series Identifiers\nECB uses SDMX format for data series:\n- Format: FLOW.FREQ.REF_AREA.INDICATOR...\n- Example for Georgia CFC: Look for series with REF_AREA=GE\n\n## Data Download Options\n\n### Web Interface:\n1. Select the data series\n2. Choose time range\n3. Click \"Download\"\n4. Select format (CSV, Excel, JSON)\n\n### Direct API Access:\n```\nhttps://data.ecb.europa.eu/api/v1/data/[DATAFLOW]/[DIMENSIONS]\n```\n\n## Calculating Depreciation Rate\n\nFormula:\n```\nDepreciation Rate (δ) = CFC(t) / K(t-1)\n```\n\nWhere:\n- CFC(t) = Consumption of Fixed Capital in year t\n- K(t-1) = Capital Stock at end of previous year (from PWT)\n\n### Excel Formula Example:\n```\n=CFC_current_year / Capital_stock_previous_year\n```\n\n## Alternative Approaches\n\nIf ECB data is unavailable for Georgia:\n1. Use PWT delta variable directly\n2. Calculate implied depreciation from capital accumulation\n3. Use regional averages (similar transition economies)\n\n### Implied Depreciation Calculation:\nFrom capital accumulation equation:\n```\nK(t) = I(t) + (1-δ) * K(t-1)\nδ = 1 - (K(t) - I(t)) / K(t-1)\n```\n\n## Typical Depreciation Rates\n- Developed economies: 4-6% annually\n- Transition economies: 5-8% annually\n- Emerging markets: 6-10% annually\n\n## Data Frequency\n- ECB typically provides annual data for national accounts\n- Some series available quarterly\n- For this analysis: Annual data needed\n\n## Notes for Georgia\n- Data availability may be limited compared to EU countries\n- National Bank of Georgia may be alternative source\n- Cross-validate with PWT delta values when possible\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":13,"cache_creation_input_tokens":952,"cache_read_input_tokens":20955,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":952,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":24},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"e5187bd3-9f2d-48b6-b06d-731e911b28ca","uuid":"043eb84d-cb91-4440-920b-a814f5a410d1"}

Find \"Consumption of fixed capital\" series\n\n### Data Series Identifiers\nECB uses SDMX format for data series:\n- Format: FLOW.FREQ.REF_AREA.INDICATOR...\n- Example for Georgia CFC: Look for series with REF_AREA=GE\n\n## Data Download Options\n\n### Web Interface:\n1. Select the data series\n2. Choose time range\n3. Click \"Download\"\n4. Select format (CSV, Excel, JSON)\n\n### Direct API Access:\n```\nhttps://data.ecb.europa.eu/api/v1/data/[DATAFLOW]/[DIMENSIONS]\n```\n\n## Calculating Depreciation Rate\n\nFormula:\n```\nDepreciation Rate (δ) = CFC(t) / K(t-1)\n```\n\nWhere:\n- CFC(t) = Consumption of Fixed Capital in year t\n- K(t-1) = Capital Stock at end of previous year (from PWT)\n\n### Excel Formula Example:\n```\n=CFC_current_year / Capital_stock_previous_year\n```\n\n## Alternative Approaches\n\nIf ECB data is unavailable for Georgia:\n1. Use PWT delta variable directly\n2. Calculate implied depreciation from capital accumulation\n3. Use regional averages (similar transition economies)\n\n### Implied Depreciation Calculation:\nFrom capital accumulation equation:\n```\nK(t) = I(t) + (1-δ) * K(t-1)\nδ = 1 - (K(t) - I(t)) / K(t-1)\n```\n\n## Typical Depreciation Rates\n- Developed economies: 4-6% annually\n- Transition economies: 5-8% annually\n- Emerging markets: 6-10% annually\n\n## Data Frequency\n- ECB typically provides annual data for national accounts\n- Some series available quarterly\n- For this analysis: Annual data needed\n\n## Notes for Georgia\n- Data availability may be limited compared to EU countries\n- National Bank of Georgia may be alternative source\n- Cross-validate with PWT delta values when possible\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}
{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-5-20251101","id":"msg_vrtx_014YshjTNoFSuLccEwZXUGGp","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_vrtx_016dteqWUFnYpzEphFLwDi6y","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/root/environment/skills/excel-hp-filter-solver.md","content":"# HP Filter Implementation in Excel with Solver\n\n## Overview\nThe Hodrick-Prescott (HP) Filter is a data smoothing technique used to separate the cyclical component of a time series from raw data. In macroeconomics, it's commonly used to extract trend GDP from actual GDP.\n\n## HP Filter Mathematical Foundation\n\n### Objective Function\nThe HP filter minimizes:\n```\nMin Σ(y_t - τ_t)² + λ * Σ[(τ_t+1 - τ_t) - (τ_t - τ_t-1)]²\n```\n\nWhere:\n- y_t = Observed series (e.g., ln(GDP))\n- τ_t = Trend component (smoothed series)\n- λ = Smoothing parameter (lambda)\n\n### Lambda Values\n- Annual data: λ = 100 (or 6.25 per Ravn-Uhlig)\n- Quarterly data: λ = 1600\n- Monthly data: λ = 14400\n\n## Excel Implementation\n\n### Step 1: Data Setup\n| Column | Content |\n|--------|---------|\n| A | Year |\n| B | Original Series (LnY or LnZ) |\n| C | HP Trend (to be solved) |\n| D | Deviation = B - C |\n| E | First Difference of Trend |\n| F | Second Difference of Trend |\n| G | Squared Second Difference |\n\n### Step 2: Calculate Components\n\n**Initial HP Trend (Column C):**\n- Start by copying Column B as initial values\n\n**First Difference (Column E):**\n```\n=C3-C2  // For row 3 onwards\n```\n\n**Second Difference (Column F):**\n```\n=E4-E3  // For row 4 onwards\n```\nOr equivalently:\n```\n=(C5-C4)-(C4-C3)\n=C5-2*C4+C3\n```\n\n**Squared Deviation (Column D squared):**\n```\n=D2^2\n```\n\n**Squared Second Difference (Column G):**\n```\n=F3^2\n```\n\n### Step 3: Objective Function\n\n```\n=SUMPRODUCT(D2:D23^2) + lambda * SUMPRODUCT(F4:F23^2)\n```\n\nOr in separate cells:\n```\nSum of Squared Deviations: =SUMSQ(D2:D23)\nSum of Squared Second Differences: =SUMSQ(F4:F23)\nObjective: =H1 + 100*H2  // for annual data\n```\n\n### Step 4: Solver Setup\n\n1. **Open Solver:**\n   - Data → Solver (or Alt+T, V)\n   - If not visible: File → Options → Add-ins → Manage Excel Add-ins → Check Solver\n\n2. **Configure Solver:**\n   - **Set Objective:** Cell containing objective function (e.g., P5)\n   - **To:** Min\n   - **By Changing Variable Cells:** HP Trend column (e.g., C2:C23 or L6:L27)\n   - **Subject to Constraints:** (usually none needed)\n\n3. **Solver Options:**\n   - Method: GRG Nonlinear (default works well)\n   - Precision: 0.000001\n   - Max Iterations: 1000\n\n4. **Click Solve**\n\n### Step 5: Verify Results\n\nAfter solving:\n- HP Trend should be smoother than original\n- Deviations should sum close to zero\n- Second differences should be small\n\n## Complete Excel Setup Example\n\n```\nRow 1: Headers\n   A      B       C        D         E         F           G\n  Year   LnZ    LnZ_HP  Deviation  1st_Diff  2nd_Diff   Sq_2nd_Diff\n\nRow 2:\n  2002  =LN(Z)  [init]   =B2-C2     -         -           -\n\nRow 3:\n  2003  =LN(Z)  [init]   =B3-C3    =C3-C2     -           -\n\nRow 4:\n  2004  =LN(Z)  [init]   =B4-C4    =C4-C3   =E4-E3      =F4^2\n\n...continue pattern...\n```\n\n## Sanity Checks\n\n1. **Before Solving:** LnZ_HP = LnZ, so:\n   - All deviations (Column D) = 0\n   - Column N (if LnA-Trend check) = 0\n\n2. **After Solving:**\n   - Trend is smooth\n   - Cyclical component (deviation) oscillates around zero\n   - No extreme values\n\n## Alternative: Built-in Functions\n\nSome Excel versions or add-ins provide HP filter functions:\n- HPFILTER (if available)\n- Third-party add-ins\n\n## Tips for Solver\n\n1. **Initial Values Matter:** Start with reasonable initial guess (copy of original data)\n2. **Scale Data:** Use logged values (ln) for better numerical stability\n3. **Check Convergence:** Solver should report \"Solution found\"\n4. **Iterations:** Increase if solver doesn't converge\n\n## Common Issues\n\n1. **Solver Not Found:** Enable in Excel Add-ins\n2. **No Convergence:** Check formulas, try different initial values\n3. **Circular Reference:** Ensure objective doesn't depend on changing cells circularly\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":13,"cache_creation_input_tokens":825,"cache_read_input_tokens":21907,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":825,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":24},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"e5187bd3-9f2d-48b6-b06d-731e911b28ca","uuid":"4dad6c95-5c76-43ed-a80d-fa4aa642abfb"}

{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-5-20251101","id":"msg_vrtx_014GpMgzH4jqTAyG8LMF2gcf","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_vrtx_01QxFTwDM8gVbo3nsTca6osi","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/root/environment/skills/cobb-douglas-production-excel.md","content":"# Cobb-Douglas Production Function in Excel\n\n## Overview\nThe Cobb-Douglas production function is widely used to represent the technological relationship between inputs (capital, labor) and output (GDP). It's fundamental for potential GDP estimation and growth accounting.\n\n## Basic Production Function\n\n### Standard Form\n```\nY = A * K^α * L^(1-α)\n```\n\nWhere:\n- Y = Output (Real GDP)\n- A = Total Factor Productivity (TFP)\n- K = Capital Stock\n- L = Labor (employment or hours)\n- α = Capital share (typically 0.3-0.4)\n- (1-α) = Labor share\n\n### Log-Linear Form\n```\nln(Y) = ln(A) + α*ln(K) + (1-α)*ln(L)\n```\n\nOr rearranged for TFP:\n```\nln(A) = ln(Y) - α*ln(K) - (1-α)*ln(L)\n```\n\n## Simplified Version (Capital Only)\n\nFor this analysis with capital emphasis:\n```\nY* = exp(ln(Z_trend)) * (K/Y)^(α/(1-α)) * Y\n```\n\nOr in terms of potential output:\n```\nY* = A_trend * K^α\n```\n\nWhere ln(Z) incorporates labor-adjusted productivity.\n\n## Excel Implementation\n\n### Step 1: Calculate Capital Share\n\nFrom PWT data:\n```\nCapital Share = 1 - Labor Share\n=1-labsh  // where labsh is from PWT\n```\n\nAverage capital share:\n```\n=AVERAGE(capital_share_range)\n```\n\n### Step 2: Calculate ln(Z) - Solow Residual\n\n```\nln(Z) = ln(Y/K^α) = ln(Y) - α*ln(K)\n```\n\nExcel formula:\n```\n=LN(Y) - alpha*LN(K)\n```\n\nOr if using Y and K directly:\n```\n=LN(B2) - $B$1*LN(C2)  // where B1 contains alpha\n```\n\n### Step 3: HP Filter ln(Z) for Trend\n\nAfter HP filtering (see HP Filter skill):\n```\nln(Z_trend) = HP_filtered_lnZ\n```\n\n### Step 4: Calculate Potential GDP (Y*)\n\n**Base Scenario:**\n```\nY*_base = exp(ln(Z_trend)) * K^α\n```\n\nExcel:\n```\n=EXP(LnZ_trend) * K^alpha\n```\n\n**With Investment Shock:**\n```\nY*_with = exp(ln(Z_trend)) * K_with^α\n```\n\nWhere K_with includes additional investment.\n\n## Capital Accumulation\n\n### Standard Formula\n```\nK(t) = I(t) + (1-δ)*K(t-1)\n```\n\n### With Investment Shock\n```\nK_with(t) = K_base(t-1)*(1-δ) + I_base(t) + ΔI(t)\n```\n\nWhere ΔI is the additional investment.\n\nExcel formulas:\n```\n// Baseline capital\n=I_base + (1-delta)*K_previous\n\n// Capital with investment shock\n=K_with_previous*(1-delta) + I_base + Investment_shock\n```\n\n## Extending K/Y Ratio\n\nWhen capital data ends before GDP projections:\n\n1. Calculate average K/Y for recent years:\n```\n=AVERAGE(K_Y_ratio_recent_years)\n```\n\n2. Project K using fixed K/Y:\n```\nK_projected = Y_projected * Average_K_Y\n```\n\nExcel:\n```\n=Y_future * $B$3  // where B3 is average K/Y\n```\n\n## Impact Calculation\n\n### Output Gap\n```\nGap = (Y*_with - Y*_base) / Y*_base * 100\n```\n\n### Cumulative Impact\n```\nCumulative = SUM(Y*_with - Y*_base)\n```\n\n### Investment Multiplier\n```\nMultiplier = Cumulative_GDP_impact / Total_Investment\n```\n\n## Excel Template Structure\n\n| Column | Variable | Formula |\n|--------|----------|---------|\n| A | Year | - |\n| B | K (Capital) | Link from PWT |\n| C | Y (GDP) | Link from WEO |\n| D | K/Y Ratio | =B2/C2 |\n| E | ln(K) | =LN(B2) |\n| F | ln(Y) | =LN(C2) |\n| G | ln(Z) | =F2-alpha*E2 |\n| H | ln(Z)_trend | HP filtered |\n| I | Investment Shock | From Investment sheet |\n| J | δK | =delta*K_previous |\n| K | K_with | =K_prev*(1-delta)+I+shock |\n| L | Y*_base | =EXP(H2)*B2^alpha |\n| M | Y*_with | =EXP(H2)*K2^alpha |\n| N | Impact | =M2-L2 |\n| O | % Impact | =N2/L2*100 |\n\n## Key Parameters\n\nTypical values:\n- α (capital share): 0.35-0.40\n- δ (depreciation): 0.05-0.08\n- λ (HP filter): 100 for annual\n\n## TREND Function for Extrapolation\n\nTo extend ln(Z) trend:\n```\n=TREND(known_y_range, known_x_range, new_x_values)\n```\n\nExample:\n```\n=TREND($H$6:$H$27, $A$6:$A$27, A28)  // For year 2024 onwards\n```\n\n## Validation Checks\n\n1. Capital share should be 0.3-0.5\n2. K/Y ratio typically 2-4 for most countries\n3. Depreciation rate 4-10%\n4. TFP trend should be smooth and growing\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":13,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1446,"cache_read_input_tokens":22732,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":1446,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":18},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"e5187bd3-9f2d-48b6-b06d-731e911b28ca","uuid":"3aa91923-91fe-4f7c-ac54-16839d874340"}
